Inspect wiring harness.Replace thermistor or pressure transducer.Field Configuration ParametersThe "Manager Menu" allows for the customization of the machine's operational envelope.Water Hardness Setting: Used to determine the frequency of the "Delime Notification".Temperature Units: Configurable between °F and °C.Auto-Shutoff (Energy Saver Mode): Programmable delay from 1 to 6 hours before the machine enters a low-power standby state.Wash Cycle Extension: Allows for the adjustment of wash times for "Pot and Pan" modes (2, 4, or 6 minutes).Maintenance Engineering and Preventive ProtocolsTo maintain the high-tolerance requirements of commercial sanitization, Hobart engineering specifies strict maintenance intervals and procedures.Deliming ProcedureChemical deliming is required to remove mineral scale from heating elements and hydraulic lines.Notification: The machine displays a delime alert based on the cycle counter and water hardness settings.Automatic Dosing (AM16/CLeN): Advansys models feature "Complete Delime," which automatically doses delimer into the booster heater and wash tank, ensuring the internal heating elements are fully de-scaled.Manual Inspection: Technicians must verify the condition of the rinse nozzles and heating elements post-delime to ensure all $CaCO_3$ deposits are removed.Filter and Screen ManagementThe filtration system is the first line of defense for the hydraulic pumps.Two-Stage Filtration: Hobart utilizes a primary scrap screen for large solids and a secondary pump intake screen for fine particles.Self-Flushing Strainer: Some models like the LT-1 utilize a self-flushing pump strainer to reduce the frequency of manual cleanings.ASR (Automatic Soil Removal): This system must be inspected daily to ensure the external scrap basket is not overflowing, as this can back up the soil-removal loop.Technical Synthesis and Engineering OutlookHobart’s engineering strategy is characterized by the convergence of mechanical robustness and digital intelligence. The progression from the AM14’s Ni-Resist impellers and electro-mechanical relays to the AM16’s CFD-optimized stainless steel wash systems and WiFi-integrated SmartConnect platforms demonstrates a focus on "Best Lifetime Value". The mechanical core—characterized by 2 HP motors delivering 160 GPM—remains the industry standard for physical soil removal, while the electronic shell provides the precision required to meet increasingly stringent Energy Star 3.0 and NSF sanitization standards.The future of Hobart engineering is clearly directed toward further energy autonomy and predictive service. Technologies such as the ventless heat-pump modules in the FT1000e series and the CLeN-VL models indicate a move toward closed-loop thermal environments that decouple the dishroom from building-wide HVAC and venting requirements. Simultaneously, the transition from float-based level monitoring to pressure transducers and digital telemetry ensures that these machines are no longer passive appliances but integrated data-generating components of the commercial kitchen ecosystem. For the engineering professional, this necessitates a dual mastery of both high-pressure hydraulic mechanics and sophisticated digital control logic to maintain the "Hobart Clean" standard across all equipment generations.

Model Database/Product Specs
-Technical Engineering Database of Hobart Commercial Warewashing Systems
The structural and functional design of Hobart commercial warewashing equipment is governed by a technical evolution spanning over a century, beginning with the acquisition of the Crescent Washing Machine Company in 1926. The engineering trajectory of these systems has transitioned from purely mechanical assemblies to complex, microprocessor-controlled environments that integrate hydraulic optimization, thermodynamic energy recovery, and sophisticated diagnostic protocols. This database provides a high-density technical analysis of Hobart’s product architecture, encompassing undercounter, door-type, rack conveyor, and flight-type systems, with a focus on engineering specifications and service parameters.Engineering Evolution and Core Architectural PrinciplesThe mechanical integrity of Hobart equipment is primarily founded on the use of 300-series stainless steel for structural frames, wash tanks, and external panels, ensuring resistance to the corrosive effects of high-alkaline detergents and thermal stress. Central to the hydraulic performance is the proprietary vertical pump design, which utilizes a self-draining configuration to prevent the stagnation of water and the accumulation of debris within the impeller housing.Thermodynamic management within these systems is dictated by the Sense-A-Temp™ technology, a thermal interlock system that monitors booster heater temperatures to ensure a minimum 180°F final rinse, as mandated by NSF standards for high-temperature sanitization. In contemporary models, this is augmented by Advansys™ technology, which utilizes energy recovery modules to capture latent heat from vapor and drainage, significantly reducing the total connected electrical load required for the booster heaters.Undercounter Warewashing Systems: Mechanical and Electronic IntegrationThe Hobart undercounter category, currently led by the LXe and LXG series, represents an engineering solution for high-density component placement within a restricted cabinet volume. These units must manage the thermodynamic requirements of high-temperature sanitization while maintaining external cabinet temperatures compatible with under-counter installation in commercial kitchens.LXe Series: Current Production EngineeringThe LXe series is segmented into the LXeH (High Temperature), LXeC (Chemical Sanitizing), and LXeR (Advansys Energy Recovery) variants. The internal architecture of the LXeR models includes a vapor eliminator and a cold-water energy recovery tank, which preheats incoming water from 55°F to approximately 110°F using heat captured from the previous cycle’s steam.Physical and Mechanical SpecificationsThe physical envelope of the LXe series is optimized for both standard and ADA-compliant installations. Standard units feature a 32.5-inch height, while the LXGeR-ADA and LXGePR-ADA models are reduced to 30.5 inches to accommodate modified countertop heights.ParameterLXeH (High Temp)LXeC (Chemical)LXeR (Advansys)Height (Standard)32.5"32.5"32.5"Width23.94"23.94"23.94"Depth25.56"25.56"26.75"Door Opening Height17.0"17.0"17.0"Rack Size20" x 20"20" x 20"20" x 20"Shipping Weight170 lbs170 lbs185 lbsTank Capacity2.9 Gallons2.9 Gallons2.9 GallonsThe hydraulic system utilizes interchangeable, snap-in revolving upper and lower wash and rinse arms. The LXi models (LXiC, LXiH) were the first to implement the digital display that communicated numeric error codes (E0, E2, E5) which have since been expanded into text-based alerts in the LXe series.Door-Type Warewashers: The AM Series Technical PlatformThe AM series, including models from the legacy AM14 to the current AM16, utilizes a hood-type design for high-volume pass-through or corner installations. The engineering focus in this series is on hydraulic pressure and automated soil management.Hydraulic Systems and Vertical Pump DesignThe heart of the AM series is a Hobart-built, heavy-duty 2 HP wash pump motor. In models like the AM14 and AM15, this motor is vertically mounted and features a Ni-Resist impeller—a high-nickel cast iron alloy chosen for its durability against abrasive detergents and cavitation.Hydraulic MetricAM14 (Legacy)AM15 (Select)AM16 (Current)Pump Motor Power1.0 HP1.5 - 2.0 HP2.0 HPPump Capacity (GPM)165160160Rinse Flow Rate (GPM)2.12.12.1Water Use (Gal/Rack)1.420.740.67Flowing Water Pressure20 ± 5 PSIG20 ± 5 PSIG20 ± 5 PSIGThe AM16 introduced the Automatic Soil Removal (ASR) system, which includes a secondary recirculating pump (8MTR) and a soil pump motor (9MTR). This system actively filters and ejects food particles into an external scrap basket throughout the wash cycle, reducing the frequency of wash water changes and increasing throughput efficiency.Thermal Regulation and Sequence of OperationsThe AM series uses a staged sequence of operations to manage the 5 kW tank heater and the optional 13 kW booster heater. The control board (AI2695) manages these loads based on inputs from temperature probes and pressure transducers.AM16 Sequence of Operations (Electric Heat)The engineering logic for the AM16 is detailed in the service manuals through a series of "K" relay activations :Standby State: Line voltage is present at transformer (1T) primary windings. 120VAC is delivered to the control board at J3.5/J3.7. The display is not lit, but logic power is active.Initial Fill: Upon pressing the "On" key, the board verifies that door switches (LS1, LS2) and the strainer interlock (LS3) are closed. The drain pump (K7) energizes for 5-second pulses to clear the sump, followed by the activation of the fill valve (K5/SOL1).Heating Sequence: Once the pressure transducer (J9) senses 0.74V, the booster heater (K1/HTR2) is energized. When the full setpoint (0.95V) is reached, the tank heater (K2/HTR1) is energized.Wash Cycle: Contactor 1CON energizes the 2 HP motor for the selected duration (1, 2, 4, or 6 minutes). In hot-water sanitizing mode, a 7-second "Sani-Dwell" occurs after the rinse to ensure thermal compliance.AM14/AM15 Gas Heat LogicFor gas-heated variants, a regulated power immersion tube system is used. A solid-state ignitor board controls the gas valve (SOL3) and provides flame ignition, with a 24V transformer stepping down the control voltage. This system includes a load-sensing switch that detects mechanical resistance. If a jam occurs, the drive motor is instantly de-energized to prevent damage to the drive pawls or the stainless steel conveyor track.Flight-Type Systems: Large-Scale Industrial Warewashing (FT1000e)The FT1000e series is designed for ultra-high-volume environments, featuring a continuous peg-belt conveyor. These machines are modular and can exceed 20 feet in length, integrating multiple wash and rinse chambers.Modular Chamber Architecture and Component LayoutA standard FT1000e Advansys machine is divided into several discrete zones, each with its own hydraulic and thermal sensors :Load Section: Features "Dish Sense" photo-eyes to detect ware and activate the final rinse pump only when dishes are present.ASR / Pre-wash: Powered by a 3.5 HP motor, this section includes the primary soil management system and a soil pump motor (9MTR).Power Wash: A high-volume chamber with a 5.0 HP motor, focused on heavy soil removal.Power Rinse: Found in e-models but omitted in the short (Se) models, this provides a secondary recirculating rinse with a 5.0 HP pump.Dual Rinse / Final Rinse: Uses a 1/6 HP pump for the dual rinse and a 1.3 GPM pumped final rinse system, which eliminates the need for external pressure gauges.Heat Pump Integration and Ventless TechnologyThe FT1000e Advansys models utilize an integrated heat pump that captures heat from the waste air and recycles it to heat the wash tank, final rinse, and the blower dryer. This technology allows the machine to operate with the lowest connected load in the industry—approximately 150 amps—saving thousands in installation wiring and circuit breaker costs.SpecificationFT1000e (Base)FT1000e (Advansys)Water Consumption58 GPH58 GPHDishes per Hour14,31014,310Conveyor Width30.0"30.0"Pre-wash Motor3.5 HP3.5 HPPower Wash Motor5.0 HP5.0 HPPower Rinse Motor5.0 HP5.0 HPExhaust Requirement1,150 CFM0 CFM (Ventless)The blower dryer, if equipped, uses a 2.0 HP fan motor and can be heated by a 7 kW electric element or a steam coil, providing forced-air drying at the exit end of the conveyor.Control and Relay Board Wire ConnectionsThe FT1000e uses a dual-board architecture consisting of a Control Board for logic and a Relay Board for power distribution.Control Board (Logic): Connects to thermistors (J1, J2), photo-eyes (Dish Sense), and door interlocks (J4). It processes signals from the 12FS ASR float switch and the 11FS dual rinse float switch.Relay Board (Power): Manages the high-voltage outputs for the wash heaters, booster heaters, and pump motors through contactors (1CON-5CON). Diagnostic LEDs on the relay board indicate the state of each contactor, facilitating rapid troubleshooting of power circuits.Control Systems and Diagnostic LogicThe reliability of Hobart warewashers is predicated on the precision of their electronic control systems. These systems have evolved from simple timing relays to the AI2695 microprocessor and the SmartConnect WiFi-enabled platforms.PCB Architecture and Connector MappingThe central control board for most AM and LX series machines is the AI2695. This alert is triggered when the machine's internal turbidity sensors or cycle counts suggest that the water quality has degraded to the point where sanitization efficacy is threatened, requiring a hard reset through a drain-and-fill cycle.Sensor Calibration and Technical ParametersThermistors (Part 00-328994) must be tested for accuracy using a high-precision ohmmeter.Temp (°F)Resistance (Ω)Engineering Application75.0100,000Baseline calibration95.063,480Warm water fill145.420,136Wash temperature check185.09,121Sanitizing rinse check212.05,569Boiling point safety tripPressure transducers are calibrated to 0.5V for an empty tank and 0.95V for a full booster tank. If the board detects a voltage outside this range during a fill cycle, it will initiate an E0 error and terminate the cycle to prevent a dry-fire of the heating elements.Technical Service Parameters and Maintenance EngineeringTo maintain the operational integrity of these machines, Hobart specifies engineering-level maintenance procedures that go beyond basic cleaning.Scale Management and Booster Guard™The high-temperature environment of the booster heater (180°F+) causes calcium and magnesium to precipitate out of the water quickly. Hobart’s Complete Delime™ with Booster Guard™ is an automated sequence that pumps a precisely metered dose of phosphoric or citric acid into the booster.Notification: The control board monitors water hardness settings (entered via the Manager Menu) and cycle counts to trigger a "Delime Recommended" alert.Procedure: During the delime cycle, the wash pump is disabled, and the delime pump (1PMP) circulates chemical through the booster for 600 seconds. This is followed by three mandatory flush cycles to ensure all acid is removed from the hydraulic path before warewashing resumes.Mechanical Component ReplacementWash Pump Seals: When replacing pump seals on the 2 HP vertical motors, the technician must ensure the carbon-ceramic seal faces are not touched by bare skin, as oils can cause premature failure. The Ni-Resist impeller must be torqued to 35 ft-lbs to maintain the dynamic balance required for 3450 RPM operation.Door Springs: The AM series utilizes spring-counterbalanced doors with nylon guides. Maintenance involves checking the tension of the stainless steel cables. If the hood does not stay in the full-open position, the springs must be adjusted at the rear mounting bracket to prevent the risk of hood-collapse injury.Programming and ConfigurationAccess to the Manager Menu (Code 1001) allows for the adjustment of cycle times, chemical dosage rates, and temperature setpoints.Cycle Selection: The AM16 can be configured for 1, 2, 4, or 6-minute wash cycles. The 4 and 6-minute cycles are NSF Pot and Pan rated and utilize a continuous wash pulse to break down baked-on soils.Chemical Priming: After chemical bottles are replaced, the "Auto-Prime" feature on the LXe and AM16 machines runs the detergent, rinse aid, and sanitizer pumps for a specific duration to purge air from the 1/4" polyethylene supply lines, ensuring consistent chemical concentrations.The technical architecture of Hobart warewashing systems is a testament to iterative engineering, where mechanical durability is enhanced by precise electronic control. From the self-draining vertical pumps to the Advansys energy recovery modules, every component is designed to operate within strict thermodynamic and hydraulic parameters to ensure the highest levels of sanitization and resource efficiency in the commercial food service environment.

Heating & Energy Systems
Engineering Analysis and Technical Database of Hobart Commercial Warewashing SystemsThe engineering of commercial warewashing equipment necessitates a precise convergence of thermodynamic management, hydraulic efficiency, and robust mechanical design to meet rigorous sanitization standards. Hobart Corporation has established a technical lineage characterized by the transition from electromechanical controls to sophisticated microprocessor-driven architectures. This report provides an exhaustive technical database and engineering analysis of Hobart’s product generations, focusing on heating systems, energy recovery mechanisms, and operational parameters across undercounter, door-type, conveyor, and flight-type platforms.Thermal Engineering and Sanitization ArchitectureThe central engineering objective in Hobart warewashing systems is the maintenance of high-temperature sanitization through controlled thermal input. The equipment must elevate incoming water to a minimum of $180^{\circ}F$ ($82.2^{\circ}C$) for the final rinse cycle to comply with NSF/ANSI standards for high-temperature machines. This is achieved through a combination of tank heaters for wash water maintenance and booster heaters for the final rinse temperature rise.Heating Methodologies and SpecificationsHobart employs three primary heating methods across its product lines: electric resistance, steam heat exchange, and gas immersion. The selection of heating type significantly influences the electrical connected load and site utility requirements.Electric Resistance HeatingElectric immersion elements are the standard for most undercounter and door-type models. In the LXe series, the electric tank heater is rated at $1.8\text{ kW}$, while the booster heater for high-temperature models (LXeH) is rated at $4.9\text{ kW}$ to achieve a $70^{\circ}F$ rise. Larger units, such as the AM16 series, utilize a $5\text{ kW}$ wash tank heater and a $13\text{ kW}$ booster heater.Model CategoryWash Tank Heat (Electric)Booster Heat (Electric)Total Connected Load (Approx. 208V/3PH)LXeR (Undercounter)$1.8\text{ kW}$$4.9\text{ kW}$$30.5\text{ Amps}$ (1PH)AM16 (Door-Type)$5\text{ kW}$$13\text{ kW}$$43.9\text{ Amps}$CLPS66eN (Conveyor)$15\text{ kW}$$30\text{ kW}$$100.5\text{ Amps}$FT1000i (Flight-Type)$56\text{ kW}$ (Total Tank)$26\text{ kW}$$150-450\text{ Amps}$Steam Heat Exchange SystemsSteam systems are utilized in high-volume conveyor and flight-type machines. These systems employ steam coils or injectors to transfer heat from a facility’s boiler system. In the CLPS66eN conveyor series, steam coils consume $65\text{ lbs/hr}$ at $10$ to $50\text{ PSI}$ flowing pressure. The condensate return requires a $10\text{ PSI}$ minimum differential pressure across the steam trap to ensure the removal of latent heat and prevent water hammer.Gas Immersion HeatingLegacy and current door-type models, such as the AM14 and AM15, offer gas-fired options. These units utilize an immersion tube burner system with a regulated power burner. The AM14C, for instance, features a gas burner rated at $25,000\text{ BTU/hr}$ with a solid-state ignitor board for automatic ignition and flame monitoring.Sense-A-Temp™ Booster TechnologyA critical mechanical component is the Sense-A-Temp™ booster heater. This system is engineered to interlock the rinse cycle with the booster temperature, ensuring that the machine does not enter the final rinse phase until the water reaches the required $180^{\circ}F$. The booster is typically constructed of stainless steel and incorporates a high-limit protector (e.g., Hobart part 00-271123) to prevent dry-firing and overheating.Energy Recovery and Thermodynamic OptimizationA major area of engineering innovation in Hobart systems is the recapture of waste energy. Modern units such as the Advansys™ models utilize thermodynamic principles to reduce the total energy input required for water heating.Advansys™ Energy Recovery and Steam EliminationThe Advansys™ system, integrated into LXeR and LXnR undercounter models, is a ventless technology that eliminates the need for an external ventilation hood. The system operates via a condensing cycle at the end of the wash process.Vapor Capture: Steam and hot water vapor are extracted from the chamber.Heat Exchange: The vapor is passed over a condenser coil filled with incoming cold water.Phase Change: The steam condenses into water droplets, releasing its latent heat of vaporization ($L_v \approx 2260\text{ kJ/kg}$) to the incoming water.Preheating: Incoming water is elevated from $55^{\circ}F$ to approximately $140^{\circ}F$ before entering the booster heater.This process reduces the booster heater's energy consumption by approximately $42\%$ and improves the kitchen's room climate by preventing humidity release.Drain Water Energy Recovery (DWER)In high-capacity conveyor models like the CLeN series, DWER technology captures sensible heat from the outgoing drain water. As hot wash water is discharged (typically at $150^{\circ}F$), it passes through a heat exchanger where it preheats incoming rinse water. Engineering data indicates this reduces rinse water heating energy by up to $20\%$ and reduces the amount of cold water required for drain water tempering (to meet plumbing codes) by up to $90\%$.Climate-Pro and Exhaust Heat RecoveryThe PREMAX and Profi flight-type series utilize the Climate-Pro energy system, which focuses on exhaust air regeneration. The system captures $100\%$ of the thermal energy in the exhaust and returns it to the rinse process, saving up to $22\text{ kWh}$ per hour. The exhaust heat recovery unit heats incoming fresh water using $60^{\circ}C$ ($140^{\circ}F$) waste steam, returning the energy via a high-efficiency heat exchanger.Mechanical and Hydraulic System ParametersThe hydraulic architecture of a Hobart dishwasher must ensure high-velocity impingement while minimizing water volume. This balance is managed through pump design, nozzle geometry, and filtration systems.Pump and Motor SpecificationsHobart utilizes heavy-duty motors, typically Totally Enclosed Fan Cooled (TEFC), with inherent thermal protection and grease-packed ball bearings.Machine TypeModelWash Pump HPPump Capacity (GPM)Motor ProtectionUndercounterLXeR$0.85$Not SpecifiedInternal ThermalDoor-TypeAM14$1.0$Not SpecifiedMagnetic SwitchDoor-TypeAM16$2.0$Not SpecifiedSolid StateConveyorC44A$2.0$ (approx.)$151$ (Weir Test)Thermal OverloadFlight-TypeFT1000i$4.0$ (Pre-Wash)Not SpecifiedTEFCThe wash pumps are designed to be self-draining to prevent bacterial growth and cross-contamination between cycles. The impellers are typically stainless steel to resist cavitation and chemical corrosion.Hydraulic Control and Opti-Rinse™Modern Hobart conveyor systems utilize the Opti-Rinse™ system, which optimizes the final rinse spray pattern. Control System & Sensors
Comprehensive Engineering Knowledge Base of Hobart Commercial Warewashing SystemsThe technical architecture of Hobart Corporation’s commercial dishwashing equipment represents a multi-generational progression from traditional electromechanical relay logic to highly integrated, microprocessor-controlled systems capable of advanced thermodynamic recovery and digital telemetry. This engineering knowledge base serves as a structured repository of product specifications, hydraulic parameters, electrical requirements, and control system logic for the door-type, conveyor, flight-type, and undercounter machine series. By synthesizing data from official service manuals, technical bulletins, and engineering datasheets, this report provides a granular examination of the mechanical and electronic frameworks that define Hobart warewashing technology.Evolution of Door-Type Systems: AM Series EngineeringThe Hobart AM series, particularly the transition from the legacy AM15 to the contemporary AM16, signifies a significant advancement in wash performance through hydraulic optimization and active soil management. The engineering focus has shifted from simple water recirculation to a complex system of filtration, energy recovery, and sensory feedback.Mechanical Design and Hydraulic OptimizationThe AM16 series introduces the X-shaped wash arms, a significant departure from previous rectangular geometries. These arms are engineered with debossed, anti-clogging nozzles designed to maximize water impact while preventing the accumulation of mineral scale within the orifices. The geometric arrangement of the nozzles on the X-arm allows for doubled water coverage over the ware compared to previous standard arms, which directly influences the mechanical force applied during the wash cycle to remove persistent food soils.The hood architecture in the AM16 models utilizes a 5-sided insulated design. This configuration is engineered to trap heat and steam more effectively than traditional 4-sided hoods, resulting in a documented 40% reduction in heat loss to the surrounding environment. This thermal retention reduces the duty cycle of the wash tank heater (HTR1) and maintains the internal ambient temperature required for consistent sanitation results.Automatic Soil Removal (ASR) MechanismsThe Automatic Soil Removal (ASR) system represents a transition from passive filtration to active centrifugal extraction. In standard machines, food soil remains in the wash water until the tank is manually drained or the strainer is cleaned. The ASR system, found on models like the AM16-ASR, utilizes an independent pump (MTR4) that actively extracts food solids from the wash tank during the operational cycle. This mechanism pumps soil into an external stainless steel scrap basket, allowing the wash water to remain significantly cleaner over a longer duration. Engineering data indicates that the ASR system reduces necessary wash-water changes by 50% and decreases pre-scrapping labor by approximately 20%.Thermodynamic Recovery: Drain Water Energy Recovery (DWER)Drain Water Energy Recovery (DWER) is a critical component of the Advansys technology suite. It functions as a liquid-to-liquid heat exchanger. Cold incoming water, typically at $55^\circ \text{F}$ to $65^\circ \text{F}$, is routed through a heat exchange coil submerged in the hot drain water ($150^\circ \text{F}$ to $160^\circ \text{F}$) before it reaches the booster heater. This pre-heating process reduces the energy required by the booster heater (HTR2) to reach the $180^\circ \text{F}$ sanitizing rinse temperature.Furthermore, the DWER system serves a dual purpose by tempering the drain water. National plumbing codes often mandate that effluent water discharged into municipal systems must be below $140^\circ \text{F}$. By extracting thermal energy to pre-heat the rinse water, the DWER system reduces the requirement for fresh cold water for tempering by up to 90%, leading to significant utility savings.Electrical Requirements and Component CalloutsThe electrical architecture of the AM16 series is designed for flexibility across various global power grids. The internal components are managed through a series of contactors (CON1, CON2) and relays (K-series) that govern the heating elements and motor cycles.Voltage/Phase/FrequencyRated AmpsMinimum Supply Circuit AmpacityMaximum Protective Device208-240V / 60Hz / 1Ph39.6 - 44.85050 208-240V / 60Hz / 3Ph23.9 - 26.73030 480V / 60Hz / 3Ph12.11515 380-415V / 50Hz / 3Ph13.52020 200-240V / 50Hz / 3Ph21.0 - 25.03030 Key electrical components identified in service documentation include:MTR1 (Wash Pump Motor): Responsible for the primary recirculation of wash water.MTR2 (Rinse Pump Motor): Drives the non-recirculating pumped final rinse to ensure consistent $180^\circ \text{F}$ delivery regardless of incoming water pressure.HTR1 (Wash Tank Heater): Maintains the wash tank at a minimum of $150^\circ \text{F}$.HTR2 (Booster Heater): A 24-30 kW element that ensures the final rinse reaches $180^\circ \text{F}$.PRS1 & PRS2 (Pressure Sensors): Electronic transducers that monitor water levels with greater precision than legacy mechanical float switches.High-Capacity Conveyor Systems: CL Series ArchitectureThe Hobart CL series (including CLeN and CL Advansys) represents modular engineering designed for high-volume environments. The architecture allows for the integration of multiple wash, rinse, and drying stages within a single conveyorized housing.Mechanical and Throughput Specifications: CL64eNThe CL64eN model serves as a representative benchmark for the modular conveyor units. It integrates a power recirculating wash section with a final rinse stage, designed for continuous operation at varying speeds.ParameterSpecification for CL64eNRacks Per Hour342 (standard 19.375" x 19.375" racks) Dishes Per Hour8,550 (average 25 per rack) Glasses Per Hour15,390 (average 45 per rack) Conveyor Speed9.5 Feet Per Minute (FPM) Maximum Chamber Opening19.5 Inches Height Wash Motor2.0 H.P. Rinse Motor2.0 H.P. Conveyor Drive Motor1/6 H.P. Combined Pump Capacity330 Gallons Per Minute (GPM) Wash Tank Capacity23 Gallons Rinse Tank Capacity22 Gallons Opti-RinSe™ Hydraulic SystemThe Opti-RinSe™ system is a patented hydraulic technology featured in the CL series. It utilizes a specific spray pattern that generates larger water droplets, which retain heat more effectively and provide greater mechanical cleaning force than standard spray patterns. This allows the machine to achieve NSF-compliant sanitation with a significantly reduced volume of final rinse water—typically $2.2 \text{ GPM}$ at $20 \text{ PSI}$. The system is integrated with the microprocessor control to ensure that the final rinse only operates when ware is present in the rinse zone, further optimizing water and energy consumption.Structural and Service ComponentsThe CL series utilizes Computational Fluid Dynamic (CFD) designed self-aligning wash manifolds. These manifolds are fitted with debossed anti-clogging wash arms that are interchangeable and easily removable for cleaning. The pumps used in these systems are stainless steel, self-draining units with integrated intake screens to protect the impellers from debris.Maintenance documentation specifies precise curtain layouts to prevent the cross-contamination of temperatures and chemicals between different zones. These curtains are categorized by length and thickness (e.g., Long STD 919758 and Short 919509) and must be installed according to model-specific templates found in the installation manuals.Flight-Type Industrial Systems: FT Series EngineeringFlight-type dishwashers, such as the FT900 and FT1000e series, are Hobart’s most complex installations, designed for massive throughput in institutional and large commercial kitchens.FT900 Engineering Metrics and Mechanical RatingsThe FT900 series utilizes a multi-tank configuration where the ware is placed directly on a 30-inch wide variable-speed conveyor belt. The mechanical ratings for the FT900 demonstrate the scale of its hydraulic and heating systems.ComponentFT900 SpecificationPre-Wash Pump150 GPM / 3.0 H.P. Motor Power Wash Pump292 GPM / 3.0 H.P. The LXGeR glasswasher is specifically optimized for bar applications, featuring an ADA-compliant 31-inch unit height.ModelCapacity (Racks/Hr)Water Usage (Gal/Rack)Cycle Time (Seconds)LXeR (Advansys)30 (Light), 24 (Normal), 13 (Heavy)0.62Light 120 / Normal 146 LXeH (Base)320.74N/A LXeC (Chemical)340.74N/A LXGeR (Glasswasher)30 (Light), 24 (Normal)0.62Light 120 / Normal 146 The LXeR and LXGeR models utilize a single, cold-water connection. Internal energy recovery systems condense steam to pre-heat the incoming water, which is then further heated by the Sense-A-Temp™ booster to reach $180^\circ \text{F}$ for sanitation.Technical Parameters for Legacy LXi ModelsThe legacy LXi series remains widely in service and utilizes a more traditional plumbing and electrical rough-in compared to the current LXe series.LXiH High Temp: Requires $110^\circ \text{F}$ minimum incoming water.LXiC Low Temp (Chemical): Requires $140^\circ \text{F}$ minimum incoming water for effective chemical reaction.Electrical Requirements: Often configured for $120/208-240\text{V}$, 3-wire systems. Rated amps range from $13.6\text{A}$ to $15.4\text{A}$ depending on the model and heater configuration.Hydraulic Limits: Required flowing water pressure is $20 \pm 5 \text{ PSIG}$. If pressures exceed $25 \text{ PSIG}$, a pressure regulating valve is mandatory.Control System Architecture and HardwareModern Hobart dishwashers utilize a sophisticated control ecosystem consisting of a main control board, extension boards for specific features, and a Human Machine Interface (HMI) for operator interaction.Main Control Board (PCB 00-975430-00001)The 00-975430-00001 control board is the primary logic engine for the AM16 and newer modular platforms. This board is responsible for processing sensor inputs and energizing the appropriate relays to drive the machine's components.Terminal Map and Pinout DetailsTechnicians utilize specific terminals on the board for troubleshooting and verification of the sequence of operations. The board architecture includes several "J" terminals for low-voltage signals and "K" relays for high-voltage switching.Terminal / RelayDescription of Engineering FunctionJ2Temperature monitoring terminal; connects to the NTC thermistor kit for wash and rinse zones.K3Energizes the Rinse Pump (MTR2) during both the rinse and the end-of-cycle drain stages.K5Energizes the Booster Fill Solenoid (SOL1) to replenish the booster tank during and after cycles.K7Energizes the Drain Pump (MTR3). It utilizes a specific timing sequence: 90s, then 30s, then 3s pulses during power-down.K8Energizes the Booster Contactor (CON1), activating the HTR2 elements.K9Energizes the Tank Contactor (CON2), activating the HTR1 elements.K10Specific to machines with Drain Water Tempering (DWT) kits; controls the 120V tempering solenoid.Extension BoardsFor machines equipped with advanced features such as ventless energy recovery or ASR, additional extension boards are daisy-chained to the main control board.ECA6 (00-975433): Primarily handles the Ventless Fan (MTR5). The FET4 output energizes the condensing fan for 20-30 seconds following the rinse cycle to eliminate steam.ECA7 (00-975431): Manages ASR (Automatic Soil Removal) and ADV models. Relay K1 on this board energizes the ASR Pump (MTR4).User Interface and HMI HardwareThe HMI (Human Machine Interface) has evolved from simple switches to durable, high-performance touchscreens (00-975443-00001) designed for the dishroom environment. The display provides real-time feedback including:Progress Bar: Visual representation of the current cycle stage.Digital Thermometers: Displays wash and rinse temperatures to $1^\circ$ precision.Error Codes: Displays specific diagnostic codes (e.g., Err 013 for timer malfunctions).Status Indicators: A tri-color LED "Play" button provides instant status: Green (Ready), Blue (Washing), and Red (Error/Service Required).Sensor Networks and Operational ParametersThe precision of modern Hobart equipment is maintained through an array of sensors that provide constant feedback to the control electronics. This closed-loop system allows the machine to adjust its operations based on real-time conditions.Temperature Sensing: NTC ThermistorsHobart utilizes Negative Temperature Coefficient (NTC) thermistors for all critical temperature monitoring points. Unlike older mechanical thermostats, these probes change resistance in a predictable curve relative to temperature changes.Temperature (∘F)Nominal Resistance (Ω)$70^\circ \text{F}$~10,000$150^\circ \text{F}$~2,500$180^\circ \text{F}$~1,200$212^\circ \text{F}$5,569 These sensors (e.g., Probe Assembly 00-328994) are used in the wash tank and the booster tank. If the resistance values fall outside a pre-programmed range, the control board will trigger a "Low Temperature Alert" or prevent the cycle from starting (Temperature Assurance).Level Monitoring: Pressure TransducersThe transition from mechanical float switches to pressure transducers (PRS1, PRS2) has significantly increased machine reliability. Mechanical floats are prone to binding due to food soil or scale. Pressure transducers measure the head pressure (weight) of the water column in the sump.Empty Tank: Typically read as a baseline voltage (e.g., $0.5 \text{V}$).Full Tank: The voltage increases as water height increases. The control board interprets this voltage to manage the fill solenoids and low-water protection for the heaters.Safety and Interlock SensorsDoor Switches (00-941429-00002): Magnetic or mechanical interlock switches that provide a signal to the board when the hood or door is closed. The machine will not enter a wash or rinse cycle if the circuit is open.ASR Basket Reed Switch (00-950437): Specifically for ASR-equipped machines, this sensor ensures the external scrap basket is in place before the soil removal pump can energize.Drains Open Indicator: Monitors the status of the drain valve to prevent heating elements from energizing in a dry tank.Digital Telemetry and NAFEM ConnectivityThe integration of the NAFEM Data Protocol (NDP) and the SmartConnect™ app suite marks Hobart’s entry into the Internet of Things (IoT) space for commercial kitchens.NAFEM Data Protocol V3.0 StandardThe NAFEM Data Protocol provides a standardized framework for data transmission between kitchen equipment and a host computer. Hobart machines compliant with this standard use specific data packets to communicate their status.Mandatory Data PacketsPacket IdentifierField LengthData IncludedEQUIP_ID95 CharactersCategory, Manufacturer ID, Serial Number, Software Version.DATA_PKT15–269 CharactersParameter Number (001-999), Payload Size, Parameter Value (e.g., Temperature).RESPONSE_PKT14 CharactersResponse code confirming success (00) or error (01-03).Optional Configuration PacketsCMD_PKT (Host Command): Allows an external host to request specific data packets or identify the machine.CONFIG_PKT (Configuration): Used by a host to write new parameters to the machine’s control board (e.g., changing a setpoint), requiring an 8-character password for security.SmartConnect™ App and Hardware InterfaceThe SmartConnect™ system utilizes the Hobart SmartConnect App to provide real-time data analysis. On the machine side, the data is often processed through a gateway module like the NDP1, which converts internal serial protocols (like MODBus) to TCP/IP over Ethernet or Wi-Fi.Features of the SmartConnect™ integration include:Sanitization Monitoring: Confirms that every cycle has reached the $160^\circ \text{F}$ plate-level temperature or the $180^\circ \text{F}$ rinse-level temperature required by NSF.Energy and Water Analysis: Aggregates consumption data to help managers optimize utility costs.Error Code Reporting: Provides immediate alerts and context for malfunctions, often including a direct link to the relevant section of the troubleshooting guide.Operational Programming and Maintenance LogicTechnicians and managers interact with the machine’s internal logic through a series of menus accessible via the control panel.The Tiered Menu SystemAccess to the machine's software is tiered to prevent unauthorized changes to critical sanitation parameters.Manager Menu (Code 1001): Accessible to the facility manager. It allows for the configuration of non-critical parameters such as language, audible alarms, and temperature units. It also contains the "Chemical Menu" for setting delime concentrations (Low, Medium, or High).Service Menu (Code 8934): Reserved for trained technicians. This menu provides access to the "I/O Test" mode, which allows the technician to manually toggle every relay and monitor every digital and analog input for hardware troubleshooting.Automatic and Manual Deliming ProceduresScale accumulation is the primary cause of heating element failure and decreased hydraulic performance. Hobart has engineered two primary deliming strategies:Manual Delime: Found on Base models. The operator initiates the cycle, and the machine provides prompts for the addition of deliming chemicals. The system then runs a specific sequence of fill, circulate, and drain cycles to strip scale.Complete Delime™ with Booster Guard™: Found on Advansys and ASR models. This system calculates scale buildup based on water hardness settings and cycle count. It then automatically pumps the delimer into both the wash tank and the booster heater, ensuring that the most critical heating components remain scale-free without operator intervention.Sequence of Operation: AM16 Door-TypeUnderstanding the engineering logic of a standard wash cycle is essential for diagnosing intermittent faults. The following is the sequence for an AM16 high-temp cycle :Standby State: Machine is powered on, water levels are verified by PRS1, and temperatures are maintained by HTR1 and HTR2.Start Command: Door switch (1LS) is closed and the Start button is pressed.Wash Cycle: Wash pump (MTR1) energizes. If equipped, the ASR system may pulse to remove heavy soil.Dwell/Drain: A brief pause allows water to shed from the ware. If the machine is programmed for a "Dirty Water" alert, the board checks the turbidity or cycle count before deciding to refresh the tank.Rinse Cycle: Rinse pump (MTR2) energizes, and the booster fill solenoid (SOL1) opens. $180^\circ \text{F}$ water is delivered to the rinse arms.Condense Stage (Ventless Only): The ventless fan (MTR5) runs for up to 30 seconds to condense steam back into water before the door lock is released.Completion: The HMI status indicator turns Green, and the door lock (SOL4) de-energizes.Service Documentation and Revision HistoriesEngineering changes are often documented through serial number breaks, which dictate the correct replacement parts and firmware versions required for a specific unit.AM16 Serial Number Revision (231270380)A critical shift in the AM16 series occurred at serial number 231270380. Machines manufactured after this threshold utilize a different set of wiring diagrams and LED indicator charts.Legacy AM16 (Prior to 231270380): Utilized a specific control board revision and was primarily documented in the original service manual Form 41321.Current AM16 (SN 231270380 to Current): Features an updated control architecture compatible with the latest SmartConnect™ hardware and utilizes updated wiring diagrams (e.g., 0F-041227 Rev B).Firmware and Software ManagementFirmware for modern Hobart equipment is managed through the Customer Support Portal. Technicians can download specific .hob or .bin files to update the machine's control logic via a USB or specialized interface. These updates often address improvements in sensor calibration, energy-saver timing, or chemical pump dosing accuracy.Preventative Maintenance and Component Life CyclesTo ensure the lifetime value of the machine, specific components require scheduled inspection and replacement based on cycle count or duration.CategoryComponentEngineering Maintenance RequirementHydraulicsWash/Rinse Manifold O-RingsInspect for tears or flattening monthly; lubricate with food-grade silicone.ChemicalsPump Squeeze TubesReplace every 6-12 months to prevent leaking or dosing inconsistency.ThermalTank/Booster ElementsCheck for scale buildup; verify amp draw matches rated specifications.MechanicalConveyor Drive Chains (CL/FT)Lubricate and check tension monthly; adjust via the actuator plate.FiltrationPump Intake ScreensMust be free of debris and correctly seated to prevent cavitation.Conclusion: Integrated Engineering for Foodservice EnvironmentsThe engineering landscape of Hobart commercial dishwashers is defined by a deep integration of mechanical durability and electronic sophistication. From the high-GPM hydraulic loops of the FT900 flight-type systems to the precision-managed temperature and pressure loops of the AM16 and LXe series, every component is designed to operate within a tight envelope of sanitation and efficiency. The shift toward digital standards like the NAFEM Data Protocol and the deployment of active soil management (ASR) and energy recovery (DWER) systems reflects an architectural commitment to reducing the total cost of ownership while maintaining the industry's highest standards for wash performance. This engineering knowledge base provides the foundational data necessary for the continued maintenance, service, and optimization of these critical kitchen assets.

Service Documentation & Engineering Diagrams
Engineering Analysis and Technical Database of Hobart Commercial Warewashing SystemsThe structural and operational architecture of Hobart commercial dishwashing equipment represents a century-long evolution in fluid dynamics, thermodynamic recovery, and electromechanical control. Since the acquisition of the Crescent company in 1926, which marked the official entry into the warewashing sector, the Hobart Corporation has transitioned from basic mechanical immersion systems to highly integrated, sensor-driven platforms like the Advansys and PREMAX lines. This transition is underpinned by the ITW Food Equipment Group’s "80/20" engineering philosophy, which prioritizes the simplification of high-impact components while renewing technological capabilities in energy and water conservation. The following technical database provides an exhaustive analysis of the mechanical, hydraulic, and electrical specifications across legacy and current product generations.Historical Engineering Evolution and Revision TaxonomyThe engineering lineage of Hobart warewashers is categorized by significant shifts in control logic and material science. The legacy era, spanning from the early 20th century to the late 1990s, relied heavily on electromechanical timers and robust, heavy-gauge stainless steel components with minimal electronic oversight. The introduction of the first flight-type dishwasher in 1953 established the blueprint for high-volume industrial sanitization, utilizing a continuous conveyor belt rather than individual racks.The integration into ITW in 1999 catalyzed the modern era of Hobart engineering. This period saw the launch of the PREMAX line in 2007, which introduced over 100 patents focused on reducing water consumption by 50% and energy by 30%. The development of the wide-angle nozzle became a fundamental mechanical revision, optimizing the spray pattern to achieve sanitization at lower flow rates. The current generation is defined by Advansys technology, which incorporates Ventless Energy Recovery (VER), Automatic Soil Removal (ASR), and Drain Water Energy Recovery (DWER).Product Generation Revision MatrixEraKey ModelsControl ArchitectureSignificant Engineering RevisionsLegacyCrescent, AM14, LXiElectromechanicalHeavy-gauge SS, Mechanical Float SwitchesTransitionalAM15, CL44/54, LXe (Early)Microprocessor (v1)Sense-A-Temp™ Boosters, Digital DisplaysCurrentAM16, CLeN, LXeR, FT1000eIntegrated HMI / IoTASR, VER, DWER, SmartConnect AppUndercounter System Architecture: LXe and LXi SeriesThe LXe and LXi series are engineered for high-density utility in restricted footprints, often requiring operation under ADA-compliant 34-inch countertops. The mechanical transition from the legacy LXi to the current LXe involved a fundamental redesign of the wash arm bearings and the chemical delivery system.LXe Series Hydraulic Parameters and Fluid DynamicsThe LXe platform utilizes a high-pressure, low-volume hydraulic circuit. The "Sense-A-Temp" booster system is a critical sub-assembly, engineered to provide a 70°F rise to incoming water. This ensures that even if the facility’s supply is only 110°F, the final rinse reaches the NSF-mandated 180°F for high-temperature sanitization. The Advansys LXeR model further optimizes this through Energy Recovery technology, which employs a heat exchanger to capture thermal energy from steam produced during the cycle, using it to preheat the subsequent fill.ParameterLXeH (High Temp)LXeC (Chemical)LXeR (Advansys)Water Consumption (GPR)0.74 gal0.74 gal0.62 galFlowing Pressure (PSIG)15 - 6515 - 6515 - 65Peak Drain Flow4.0 GPM4.0 GPM4.0 GPMIncoming Water Temp (Min)110°F120°F55°F (with VER)Racks Per Hour (Normal)323424The LXeR’s ability to utilize cold water (55°F) for the initial fill is a significant deviation from standard engineering requirements, made possible by the heat recovery coil. The PuriRinse feature in low-temperature Advansys models (LXePR) introduces a secondary fresh-water rinse after the chemical sanitization phase to remove residual sodium hypochlorite, which can affect the head retention of beer and the flavor profile of carbonated beverages.Electrical Infrastructure for Undercounter UnitsThe LXe series supports multiple electrical configurations to accommodate varying facility infrastructures. The HMI (Human-Machine Interface) is a top-mounted digital display that eliminates the need for manual gauges by utilizing high-accuracy thermistors to report tank and booster temperatures.Door-Type System Engineering: AM Series (AM15 and AM16)The AM series represents the heavy-duty vertical-chamber architecture. The AM15 served as the baseline for high-volume door-type units, featuring a 2 H.P. wash motor and a 160 GPM pump capacity. The AM16 series introduced the X-shaped wash arm, which effectively doubles the water coverage per rotation compared to the standard straight-arm design.Mechanical and Material SpecificationsThe construction of the AM15 and AM16 utilizes 304-series stainless steel for all wetted surfaces. The frame is 12-gauge, while the drawn tank and chamber panels are 16-gauge and 18-gauge respectively. This material choice is governed by the need for resistance to both thermal cycling and the corrosive effects of concentrated detergents.ComponentTechnical SpecificationEngineering DetailWash Motor2 H.P.TEFC design, inherent thermal protectionPump Capacity160 GPMWeir test certified, self-drainingPump SealCarbon-CeramicStainless steel spring-loaded interfaceTank Heater5 kW (Electric)Incoloy immersion elementOptional Gas Heat25,000 BTU/HrInfrared immersion tube systemWash Tank Capacity14 GallonsDrawn stainless steel, no internal weldsThe "Ratio Fill" method in the AM series is a critical operational parameter. Unlike legacy systems that utilized mechanical floats prone to scaling and sticking, the AM16 uses a microcomputer-controlled solenoid valve. The system calculates the fill volume based on a timed opening of the valve, adjusted for the building’s flowing water pressure (minimum 20 PSIG).AM16 Automatic Soil Removal (ASR) SubsystemThe ASR system is a revolutionary mechanical addition to the AM16 platform. It consists of a dedicated 1/4 H.P. pump and a specialized scrap basket. During the wash cycle, a portion of the recirculating water is diverted through the ASR assembly to carry heavy solids out of the wash tank and into an external scrap basket. This process maintains the clarity of the wash water, reducing the number of daily tank changes and preserving the thermal energy of the heated water.Conveyor Architecture: CL and CLe SeriesHobart’s CL series dishwashers are designed for modularity, allowing configurations for single-tank, two-tank, and ventless operations. The engineering architecture is centered on a continuous chain-drive conveyor system moved by a 1/6 H.P. to 1/3 H.P. motor (MTR1).Operational Sequence and Logic GatesThe CLe series utilizes a complex logic sequence managed by the primary control board. The sequence is gated by multiple safety interlocks, including door reed switches and table limit switches.Initialization: Upon pressing the "On" key, the HMI checks the status of the pressure sensors (PRS1 for booster, PRS2 for wash tank). If the voltage is below 0.5V, the system initiates a fill cycle.Fill Cycle: The system differentiates between "Empty Tank Fill" and "Full Tank Top-off." During the empty tank fill, the fill valve (SOL1) and rinse pump (MTR3) operate until the tank pressure sensor reaches the set-point voltage (typically 1.5V to 2.0V).Heating Phase: Once water levels are confirmed, the control board energizes the heater contactors (CON1/CON2). The system prioritizes booster heat over tank heat to ensure sanitization readiness.Wash/Rinse Cycle: The conveyor motor activates only after the wash tank reaches its minimum set temperature (150°F for high-temp units). The final rinse is triggered as the rack breaks the infrared beam or mechanical arm at the machine exit.Motor and Pump Specifications for CL SeriesMotor DesignationFunctionHP / PhaseFull Load Amps (FLA)MTR1Conveyor Drive1/6 - 1/3 HP / 3PH0.8 - 1.2 AMTR2Wash Pump2 HP / 3PH5.8 - 6.4 AMTR3Final Rinse Pump1/2 HP / 1PH2.3 AMTR5Prewash Pump1 HP / 3PH3.2 AMTR11Delime PumpPeristalticNegligibleThe CL series also incorporates a "Drain Water Tempering" (DWT) mechanism. This involves a thermistor mounted at the drain manifold that triggers a cold-water solenoid if the discharge temperature exceeds 140°F, ensuring compliance with plumbing codes that prevent thermal damage to PVC sewer lines.Industrial Industrial Flight-Type Systems: FT1000eThe FT1000e series is the largest warewashing platform manufactured by Hobart, designed for throughputs exceeding 14,000 dishes per hour. The engineering of the FT1000e is inherently modular, consisting of a load section, prewash, power wash, power rinse, dual rinse, and an unload section.Mechanical and Thermodynamic EngineeringThe FT1000e utilizes "Ventless Technology," which is an integrated heat pump system. This system captures the latent heat from the exhaust air and recycles it to heat the wash tank water and the blower-dryer air. This drastically reduces the total connected load, with modern units requiring only 150 amps compared to over 300 amps in previous generations.FeatureSpecificationEngineering BenefitConveyor Width30 InchesAccommodates 18"x26" bun pansVariable Speed4.0 - 8.5 FPMAllows adjustment for soil levelsPrewash Motor3-1/2 H.P. Legacy flight-type machines required technicians to manually remove end caps to clean debris; the current Hobart design utilizes a sliding manifold that can be flushed without disassembly, reducing service time by an estimated 40%.Electronic Architecture and Control SystemsModern Hobart machines utilize a unified control platform that integrates the HMI, power distribution, and sensor feedback. The core of this system is the primary control board, often supplemented by extension boards (EC A6 and EC A7) for complex configurations like the AM16 or FT1000e.Sensor Calibration and Voltage RangesThe diagnostic reliability of the Hobart platform is contingent upon the accuracy of its pressure and temperature sensors. These sensors provide an analog DC voltage to the control board, which is then digitized for processing.Sensor TypeApplicationAcceptable Voltage RangePressure (PRS1)Booster Water Level0.5V - 1.0V DCPressure (PRS2/4)Wash/Prewash Level0.5V - 2.0V DCThermistor (QTM1)Booster Temperature10k Ohm @ 77°F (Nominal)HMI InterfaceUser Controls5V DC Logic LevelThe "SmartConnect" app integration is achieved through a Wi-Fi module on the control board. Technicians can access the Manager Menu using PIN 1001 to view connection status or perform a "Firmware Update" via a.hob or.bin file uploaded through the service port.Sequence of Operations: Fill and Heating Logic (CLe/AM16)The logic gate for heating is strictly interlocked with the water level sensor.Low Water Safety: If the pressure sensor (PRS) reports a voltage below 0.4V, the control board interrupts the circuit to the heater contactor (CON). This prevents "dry-firing" of the Incoloy elements, which would otherwise reach critical failure temperature within seconds.Ratio Fill Calibration: The system monitors the rate of voltage increase during the fill. If the voltage does not increase by a specific delta within 60 seconds, the HMI triggers a "FILL ERROR," assuming a clogged inlet strainer or a failed fill solenoid (SOL).Temperature Priority: In high-temp models, the rinse booster heater (HTR2) is given priority. If the booster and tank both require heat, the tank heater is cycled off to provide full amperage to the booster, ensuring that the machine is ready for the 180°F final rinse as soon as the cycle is initiated.Service Diagnostics and Error Code DatabaseThe Hobart diagnostic framework is designed to provide granular fault isolation. Error codes are displayed on the HMI and are often accompanied by a wrench icon for critical faults.Comprehensive Error Code Reference TableError CodeMeaningProbable CauseService ProcedureE0, E2, E3, E4Fill ErrorClogged strainer, closed valveClean Y-strainer; check solenoid coilE5Drain FaultKinked hose, jammed pumpClear impeller; verify drain pathE6CommunicationCable failure, PCB faultReseat HMI cables; check 5V railCL/PrOverfill/LeakDirty probes, solenoid leakClean probes with Scotch-BriteWASH ARM ALERTObstructionClogged nozzles, stuck armRemove and flush wash armsLOW RINSE TEMPThermal LagSupply water <110°FVerify building heater; check contactorProbe Err - TANKSensor FailurePRS2 damaged or unpluggedMeasure voltage at PRS2; check harnessF01 (1&2)Booster SensorShorted/Open thermistorReplace QTM1; check for moisture in plugF02 (1&2)Tank SensorShorted/Open thermistorReplace QTM2SIEStrainer FaultMagnet missing or misalignedReinstall scrap basket; check reed switchFIL1/2/3Fill TimingLow flow, high pressureCheck flow rate; adjust regulatorALDrain Fault (FX)Jammed pump, kinked hoseInspect pump cavity for debrisHEIThermostopBooster heater failureCheck resistance of HTR2 elementsULOverflowLeaky fill valve, blocked air trapReplace SOL1; clean pressure hoseThe "WASH ARM ALERT" is a second-order diagnostic feature found in LXe and AM16 models. The control board monitors the current draw of the wash pump motor. If the current fluctuates beyond a specific frequency, it indicates that the wash arm is not rotating at the correct RPM, usually due to a physical obstruction or a nozzle clogged with heavy debris.Installation Engineering and Infrastructure RequirementsInstallation of Hobart equipment must adhere to the National Electrical Code (NFPA 70) and the National Fuel Gas Code (ANSI Z223.1).Plumbing and Water Quality ParametersHobart engineering requires specific water quality to prevent premature component failure. Hardness exceeding 3 grains per gallon will lead to rapid scale accumulation on the booster elements, which acts as an insulator and causes the element to burn out.SpecificationRequirementImpact of Non-ComplianceWater Hardness< 3 Grains/GallonElement failure, clogged nozzlesChloride Level< 50 PPMPitting and stress corrosion of SS tankFlowing Pressure15 - 65 PSIGHigh pressure damages solenoid valvesDrain Pipe Size2" FPT (AM/CL)Slow drainage, backup into wash tankHot Water Supply110°F MinCycle time increases due to heating lagFor gas-heated models (AM15G, CL44G), the gas supply pressure must be regulated. Natural gas should not exceed 7" W.C. (Water Column), and Liquid Propane (LP) must not exceed 11" W.C.. The exhaust flow requirements for these units are critical; the CL series typically requires 200 CFM at the entrance and 400 CFM at the exit to effectively remove combustion byproducts and moisture.Ventilation and ClearancesRequirementUndercounter (LXe)Door-Type (AM16)Conveyor (CL)Wall Clearance0" - 1"0"18" (Service Side)Ceiling HeightN/A80" Min (Door Open)12" Above MachineService Clearance39-1/2" (Front)30" (Front/Side)35" (Strainer Removal)VentilationOptional (Advansys)Hood RequiredHood RequiredAdvansys units with Ventless Energy Recovery utilize a cold water coil to condense steam. The engineering implication is that while an exhaust hood is not required by NSF, the room's HVAC system must be capable of handling the latent heat load rejected by the machine’s chassis, which is approximately 3,500 - 5,000 BTU/Hr depending on the model.Maintenance Engineering and Component LifecycleThe Hobart service manual specifies rigorous maintenance parameters to ensure the 20-year lifecycle typical of the brand.Lubrication and Mechanical AdjustmentsWash Arm Bearings: The interchangeable upper and lower wash arms utilize a stainless steel ball-bearing assembly. This must be inspected for flat spots every 1,000 cycles.Conveyor Chain Tension: In CL and FT models, the drive chain tension must be checked monthly. Over-tensioning leads to premature wear of the drive motor bearings (MTR1), while under-tensioning can cause the chain to jump the sprocket, triggering a "Conveyor Jam" error.Vacuum Breakers: Mounted 6 inches above the highest rinse opening, the vacuum breaker prevents siphoning of wash water into the potable supply. Service procedures require the disassembly and cleaning of the brass seat every 6 months to prevent lime scale from causing the valve to stick open.Deliming Sequence EngineeringThe "Auto Delime" logic in the Advansys series is a timed maintenance gate. Based on the water hardness programmed into the Manager Menu, the machine will prompt the user to initiate a delime cycle. During this cycle:The machine drains the wash tank and refills it with fresh water.The delime pump (MTR11) injects a specified volume of phosphoric or methanesulfonic acid into the tank.The wash pump runs for 10-20 minutes to circulate the acid through the booster and wash arms.The system performs three consecutive rinse-and-drain cycles to ensure no acid residue remains.This process is critical for machines with Sense-A-Temp boosters. Scale buildup of just 1/16th of an inch can increase the energy consumption of the booster by 15%, as the heating element must work harder to transfer heat through the mineral layer.Conclusion of Engineering DatabaseThe Hobart Corporation's warewashing equipment represents a highly sophisticated integration of mechanical durability and electronic efficiency. From the 2 H.P. motors of the AM series to the 6 H.P. heat pump compressors in the FT1000e, every component is engineered to a specific duty cycle that balances sanitization requirements with resource conservation. The transition to the Advansys platform has shifted the service burden from mechanical troubleshooting to digital diagnostics, requiring technicians to have a nuanced understanding of sensor voltages, HMI logic, and thermodynamic recovery cycles. This technical database serves as the authoritative engineering reference for the design, installation, and maintenance of these critical industrial systems.

Comprehensive Engineering Database and Technical Analysis of Hobart Commercial Warewashing SystemsThe structural and mechanical engineering of commercial warewashing systems by Hobart Corporation represents an iterative legacy of thermodynamic efficiency, hydraulic optimization, and sophisticated electronic control logic. The product line is categorized by operational throughput and mechanical architecture, ranging from compact undercounter units to large-scale industrial flight-type systems. This research report details the technical specifications, architectural standards, and service parameters of Hobart's commercial dishwashing equipment, prioritizing manufacturer-level engineering documentation and technical bulletins.Architectural Classification and Product NomenclatureThe engineering design of Hobart warewashers is predicated on a modular nomenclature system that identifies machine types and performance tiers. This taxonomy is essential for service professionals and system designers to identify the specific mechanical and electronic capabilities of a unit based on its model prefix and suffixes.Performance Tier IdentifiersThe Hobart engineering standards differentiate between the PROFI and PREMAX lines. The PROFI tier provides high-efficiency standard equipment, including GENIUS-X² fine filter systems, drain pumps, and chemical dispensing. The PREMAX tier represents the technological ceiling of Hobart engineering, incorporating exclusive features such as integrated drying (TOP-DRY), steam condensation (VAPOSTOP²), and Permanent-Clean soil removal systems.IdentifierTechnical DefinitionEngineering Implementation and ImplicationsGGlasswasherOptimized for fragile ware and high-clarity rinse performance. FFront-loadingStandard undercounter architecture with integrated pump systems. UUtensil washerHigh-pressure hydraulics for heavy soil removal from pans and crates. AMHood-typeVertical pass-through operation using a 5-sided or 4-sided hood. AUHigh-pressure HoodSpecialized hood units with enhanced impingement for utensils. XPROFI EquipmentStandardizes Genius-X² filtration and automated dosing. PPREMAX FeaturesHighest efficiency; includes steam rinsing and advanced soil evacuation. XXROTOR-X Wash SystemDual-arm rotating wash manifolds for maximized water coverage. CCompactReduced footprint for space-constrained kitchen layouts. SSoftenerIntegrated ion-exchange resins for water hardness management. LLarge / XLExpanded chamber height or width for gastronorm pans and crates. TTwin / DoubleDual-rack capacity within a single machine footprint. RHeat RecoveryPassive or active systems recycling drain or exhaust heat. The materials used in construction are typically 304-series or 1.4301-grade stainless steel for tanks, frames, and wash arms, ensuring structural integrity and corrosion resistance in high-temperature, chemically aggressive environments.Door-Type Dishwashing Systems: AM Series EngineeringThe AM Series represents the core of Hobart’s medium-volume offerings, designed for hospitality environments processing between 60 and 80 racks per hour. The engineering evolution of this series has focused on hydraulic coverage and soil management.AM16 Architecture and Hydraulic DesignThe AM16 is the current flagship of the door-type category. Its primary mechanical advancement is the X-shaped wash arm design, which doubles the water coverage over standard single-arm configurations. The hydraulic system is powered by a 2.0 HP high-efficiency pump with a weir-test rating of 160 Gallons Per Minute (GPM).For the Advansys™ and ASR (Automatic Soil Removal) variants, the engineering logic incorporates a dedicated pump (MTR4) that actively evacuates food soil from the wash tank before it can break down and contaminate the recirculating solution. This system maintains water clarity for a longer duration, reducing the frequency of required tank changes by up to 50%.AM16 Technical Specifications and Operational ParametersThe AM16 is engineered to meet NSF/ANSI Standard 3, achieving a 160° F sanitization temperature at the dish level for high-temperature models. A unique engineering trait of the AM16 is the absence of a mechanical pressure gauge in the pumped rinse system; instead, it utilizes transducers and millivolt-based pressure sensors to monitor hydraulic performance.CategoryParameterSpecification DetailCapacityRacks per Hour60 (Standard) to 80 (AMTL Two-Level). Dishes per Hour1,450 to 1,625 (Average 25 per rack). HydraulicsWash Pump2.0 HP; 160 GPM Weir Test rating. Wash ArmX-shaped, interchangeable, anti-clogging nozzles. TemperaturesWash Set-point150° F (Minimum). Rinse Set-point180° F (Hot Water Sanitizing). ConsumptionWater per Rack0.74 Gallons (Standard) or 0.62 Gallons (Advansys). DimensionsHood Opening17 inches (Standard) to 20.75 inches (XL/Utensil). Footprint27" W x 28.5" D x 66.5" H (Standard). Revision Control and Service IdentificationHobart engineering documentation tracks major design changes by serial number. For the AM16, a critical transition occurred at Serial Number 231270380, which marked changes in the electrical architecture and firmware requirements. Units manufactured after this serial number require specific.hob or.bin firmware files for the control board to manage updated sensor inputs and relay logic.The control board (A1) for the AM16 manages several critical components through a defined connector map. PRS1 and PRS2 provide millivolt signals from the booster and wash tank pressure sensors to determine water levels, replacing traditional mechanical floats which are prone to fouling. Thermistors (QTM1, QTM2, QTM3) monitor temperatures in the wash tank, booster, and final rinse system, allowing the control logic to extend wash cycles (Thermostop) if temperatures are below NSF minimums.Rack Conveyor Systems: CL Series Technical AnalysisThe CL Series (CLe and CL Advansys) is engineered for high-capacity environments, utilizing a modular design that scales cleaning power based on machine length. The units are available in 44" (CL44) through 86" (CL86) configurations.CL Series Hydraulic and Conveyor MechanicsThe mechanical core of the CL series is the "Rapid Return" conveyor drive mechanism. This system uses a patented ball detent clutch to provide maximum protection against conveyor jams, which could otherwise damage the drive motor or the racks themselves.The wash manifolds are designed using Computational Fluid Dynamics (CFD) to be self-aligning. This ensures that the water jets from the stainless steel, debossed anti-clogging nozzles strike the ware at the optimal impingement angle. The hydraulic system also features "Capless Wash Arms," which eliminate the traditional removable end-caps. This design choice prevents pressure loss from missing caps and simplifies the daily cleaning procedure.CL Series Technical SpecificationsModel IdentifierLengthTank Capacity (Wash)Motor Power (Wash)Racks per HourCL44 / CL44e44 inches23 Gallons2.0 HP202 CL64 / CL64e64 inches23 Gallons2.0 HP342 CL66 / CL66e66 inches23 Gallons2.0 HP202 - 342 CL86 / CL86e86 inches23 Gallons2.0 HP342 The CL64 and CL66 models often include a pre-wash module. In the CL64eN gas-heated variant, the machine consumes 78,000 BTU/hr per tank for heating, whereas electric versions utilize a 15kW tank heater. The conveyor speed for high-throughput models is rated at 9.5 feet per minute.Electrical Architecture and Power DistributionThe CL series provides options for Single Point or Dual Point electrical connections. In a Single Point configuration, all motors, controls, and the booster heater are wired to one terminal block. Dual Point connections are typically field-converted and separate the booster heater (30kW) into its own circuit.Electrical Load Requirements (CL66-ADV Electric example):208/60/3: 115.7 Rated Amps; 150 Amp Min Supply Circuit. 240/60/3: 105.1 Rated Amps; 125 Amp Min Supply Circuit. 480/60/3: 54.7 Rated Amps; 70 Amp Min Supply Circuit. 600/60/3: 43.4 Rated Amps; 60 Amp Min Supply Circuit. Heat dissipation for these units is significant, with a CL44-ADV model releasing 32,100 BTU/HR of latent heat and 13,700 BTU/HR of sensible heat into the environment, necessitating robust HVAC integration.Flight-Type Systems: FT Series Engineering ArchitectureThe FT1000e and FT1000Se represent the industrial-scale engineering tier of Hobart dishwashers. These machines are designed for continuous high-volume operations, such as hospitals and flight kitchens, washing over 14,000 dishes per hour.Modular Configuration and Hydraulic FlowThe architecture of the FT series is built around massive 40-gallon modules. A typical FT1000e configuration includes a Pre-Wash, Power Wash, and Power Rinse module, each powered by a 3.5 HP Totally Enclosed Fan Cooled (TEFC) pump.Module TypeTank Volume (Gal)Pump HPPurposePre-Wash403.5 HPInitial soil removal and wetting. Power Wash403.5 HPHigh-impingement chemical cleaning. Power Rinse403.5 HPChemical removal and heat transfer. ASR Module7.21/3 HPActive soil evacuation. Dual Rinse7.21/6 HPFinal sanitizing rinse application. The conveyor belt is 30 inches wide, supporting variable speeds from 4.0 to 8.5 feet per minute. The chamber height is 22 inches, accommodating 18" x 26" bun pans.Thermodynamic Management and Heat RecoveryThe FT1000e Advansys models utilize a heat pump system to recycle energy. In steam configurations, a 6 HP compressor is used to extract thermal energy from the exhaust air and return it to the wash tank. This system enables the machine to maintain a 160°F wash temperature with minimal external steam consumption, which is rated at 138 lbs/hr for the entire machine.Incoming water temperatures are managed through an Energy Recovery module that pre-heats fresh intake water to approximately 110°F using waste heat. The final rinse consumption is highly optimized at 58 gallons per hour (GPH), or 0.97 gallons per minute (GPM).Electrical Connectivity and ServiceThe FT1000e requires sophisticated power distribution, often utilizing multiple connection points. For a 480V/60/3 system, the single-point connection is rated at 67.1 Amps. Multi-point configurations separate the motors/blower dryer (E1), heat pump (E3), and electric booster (E5/E6).Firmware updates for the FT series are managed via.bin files and specific revision controls for wiring sheets (e.g., Rev B to Rev D). Service professionals must verify the serial number range (e.g., Prior to 271196738 vs. 271197621 to Current) before performing diagnostic tests.Undercounter and Glasswashing: LX and PREMAX TechnologyThe LX Series has evolved through the LXi (Legacy), LXe (Advanced), and LXn (Current) generations. The focus of this evolution has been the reduction of water consumption and the integration of smart diagnostics.LX Series Mechanical EvolutionThe LXi series established the standard with a 1.2-gallon water consumption per rack. The LXe series improved this to 0.74 gallons, and the Advansys LXeR variant further reduced consumption to 0.62 gallons. The current LXn series maintains the 0.62-gallon rating while introducing a color touchscreen and integrated WiFi for SmartConnect app monitoring.ModelTypeRacks/HourWater/RackWash MotorLXiCChemical301.2 Gal 3/4 HPLXiHHigh Temp301.2 Gal 3/4 HPLXeCChemical340.74 Gal 0.85 HPLXeHHigh Temp320.74 Gal 0.85 HPLXeRAdvansys30 (Light)0.62 Gal 0.85 HPLXnHHigh Temp32 (Light)0.62 Gal 0.85 HPThe mechanical design of the LX series features a 17-inch door opening, which allows for the cleaning of large prep-ware, mixing bowls, and cafeteria trays. The current LXn series includes a "two-level" rack capability, which can boost throughput to 48 racks per hour by washing small items on an upper rack while simultaneously washing plates on the lower rack.PREMAX FP Undercounter EngineeringThe PREMAX FP is a premium European-engineered undercounter model. It incorporates TOP-DRY integrated drying, which utilizes a dedicated blower and heating system to eliminate manual polishing. The VAPOSTOP² system is a key engineering safety feature that condenses steam before the door is opened, preventing steam clouds from entering the kitchen.PREMAX FP Technical Data:Wash Pump: 0.5 kW; 350 L/min flow rate. Filtration: Genius-X² fine filter with pre-wash function. Consumption: 0.8 Liters per rack in ECO mode; 2.0 Liters in standard mode. Wash Tank: 10.6 Liters (approx. 2.8 Gallons). Electrical Load: 6.7 kW (415/50/3) switchable to 3.2 kW (240/50/1). The PREMAX FP also features the 2-in-1 double rack system and SENSO-ACTIVE resource management, which constantly monitors wash water quality using turbidity sensors to adjust the amount of fresh rinse water required.Electrical Control Logic and Sub-systemsThe transition from mechanical timers to modular control boards (PCB) has redefined Hobart's service parameters. The modern Hobart control system is built around the Smartronic or VISIOTRONIC interfaces.Centerline Control Board (A1) and Connector MappingThe Centerline series and newer door-type machines utilize a standardized control board (A1). The connector map defines the relationship between sensors and actuators.Connector IDEngineering FunctionTechnical ContextCN5-2 / CN5-3Wash PumpOutputs power to the 1MTR wash pump motor. CN7A / CN7CPower/FusingConnects main power through 1FU fuse to the board. CN9-1Fill ValveActuates the water solenoid for automatic tank fill.
