Power is typically derived from a transformer (1T-X1) that provides 120VAC to the board on CN9-7, which is then routed through a PF1 fuse (2FU) to power the tank and booster heat relays.Programming and Parameter ConfigurationTechnicians can access the configuration menu to set machine parameters. In the Centerline dishwasher, a technician enters the menu by holding "Menu" and "Delime" buttons simultaneously with the door open.Parameter 23: Sets the dishwasher program type (01 for Cold Unit/CUL, 02 for Hot Unit/CUH).Parameter adjustments: Cycles are set to light, normal, or heavy, which adjusts the wash and dwell times.Thermostop: A programmable lockout that prevents the rinse cycle from starting until the booster heater reaches the 180°F set-point.Sequence of Operations (AM16 example)The engineering logic for a standard wash cycle is as follows :Power Initialization: The HMI checks the status of the door switch (LS1) and the scrap basket reed switch (LS2).Fill Cycle: The fill solenoid opens. If the water level is below the transducer set-point, the booster and tank heaters are disabled until the minimum level is sensed.Preheat Cycle: The booster (HTR2) and tank (HTR1) heaters cycle to reach their respective set-points.Wash Phase: The wash pump motor (MTR1) runs for the selected duration (60, 120, 240, or 360 seconds).ASR Phase: If equipped, the ASR pump (MTR4) activates to purge accumulated soil.Rinse Phase: The rinse pump (MTR2) activates, forcing booster water through the final rinse arms.Drain Phase: Upon shutdown, holding the power button for 5 seconds initiates a powered drain cycle.Hydraulic Engineering and Soil ManagementHobart's hydraulic design focuses on the "impingement" of water on the ware. This is achieved through the relationship between pump horsepower and wash manifold nozzle design.Pump Systems and ImpellersWash pumps are typically self-draining and constructed of stainless steel to minimize bacterial growth.Standard Wash Pump: 2.0 HP, 160 GPM Weir Test rating.LXi/LXe Pump: 3/4 HP to 0.85 HP, with a 54 GPM capacity.FT Series Pump: 3.5 HP per module.PREMAX Wash Pump: 0.5 kW (approx. 2/3 HP), delivering 350 L/min (approx. 92 GPM).The impellers are debossed and anti-clogging, designed to handle high volumes of particulate matter before it reaches the fine filter system.Filtration and Soil Recovery SystemsThe engineering of the Genius-X² filter system allows for continuous cleaning of the wash water. The system follows a four-stage process:Coarse Strainer: Captures large debris like bone fragments and pits.Fine Filter: Traps small food particles during the recirculating wash cycle.Soil Sensing: Turbidity sensors monitor water clarity (Senso-Active) and trigger extra rinse water if needed.Pre-flush: During the final rinse, the fresh water flush is used to push trapped debris out of the fine filter and into the drain, ensuring the next wash cycle starts with cleaner water.The Automatic Soil Removal (ASR) system in door-type and conveyor models uses a dedicated pump to pull soil from the bottom of the scrap screen and pump it directly to the drain, reducing the "dirty water" indicator activation.Thermodynamic Systems: Heat Recovery and BoostersMaintaining a consistent 180°F (82°C) sanitizing rinse is the most energy-intensive process in a dishwasher. Hobart engineering utilizes several systems to mitigate this load.Drain Water Energy Recovery (DWER)DWER is a passive heat exchange system. Hot wash water exiting the machine at approximately 150°F is routed through a heat exchanger. Cold incoming water (55°F) is routed through the other side of the exchanger.Efficiency Gain: Pre-heats intake water to 110°F-120°F without additional electricity.Tempering: Simultaneously cools the drain water to meet plumbing codes that require drain water to be below 140°F (reducing the need for separate cold-water tempering kits by up to 90%).Steam and Booster EngineeringHobart boosters are generally "pressure-less" and constructed of 304 stainless steel.Standard Electric Booster: 18kW to 30kW, capable of providing a 70°F rise in water temperature.Steam Booster: Requires 20 PSI steam pressure to raise 130°F entering water to 180°F.Booster Guard: Automatically doses delimer into the booster heater during the delime cycle, preventing scale buildup on the heating elements.Ventless Energy Recovery ModulesIn ventless units, a blower dryer (typically 2 HP) or fan channels hot water vapor through a condenser coil.Evaporator Coil: Cold refrigerant or water pulls heat from the steam.Phase Change: The steam condenses into liquid water, which is drained.Energy Return: The captured heat is used to pre-heat the next fill cycle, saving up to $6,700 in annual energy costs on industrial flight-type units.Legacy Model Data and Mechanical ParametersMaintaining legacy Hobart equipment requires access to mechanical specifications for models no longer in production, such as the AM14, AM15, and CLe series.AM15 Series (Legacy AM Select)The AM15 was a high-performance door-type unit utilizing the "Ratio Fill" method. This logic controlled the fill valve based on timed intervals to ensure correct water levels regardless of varying building flow pressures.ComponentSpecificationWash Motor2 HP, inherently protected. Tank Heat5kW Electric or 25,000 BTU/hr Gas. Capacity58 racks/hour (High Temp), 65 racks/hour (Low Temp). Dimensions27" W x 28.5" D x 66.5" H. Rinse Time10 seconds per rack. CL44e Rack Conveyor (Legacy CLe Series)The CL44e was a single-tank rack conveyor rated at 202 racks per hour. It featured flexible thermal layer curtains at the entry and exit to prevent heat loss.CLe Engineering Features:Pump Capacity: 165 GPM Weir Test rating.Tank Volume: 23 Gallons.Final Rinse Flow: 2.1 GPM at 20 PSI flowing pressure.Field Convertibility: Technicians could convert the unit between high-temp and low-temp sanitizing modes via a software change in the microcomputer control module.Service, Maintenance, and Calibration ProtocolsThe long-term reliability of Hobart equipment is dependent on adherence to standardized service and maintenance parameters.Installation and Leveling RequirementsUnits must be placed on a level surface to ensure proper drainage and door seal integrity. On LXi/LXe undercounter units, the machine is leveled using a diagonal track measurement.Service Clearances: Conveyor models require a minimum of 20 inches of clearance on both sides and 23 inches of front clearance for component access.Rough-in Heights: Hot water fill (P2) is typically 11-13/16" AFF, and the drain (P1) is 7-3/8" AFF for CL series units.Maintenance Procedures and Component ServiceRegular maintenance is categorized by daily and monthly tasks.Wash/Rinse Arms: Remove thumb nuts, pull arms off shafts, and inspect nozzles for blockage. Anti-clogging nozzles should be cleared with a non-metallic tool.Water Level Probes: These probes sense impedance to determine tank level. They must be cleaned with a non-metallic scouring pad (e.g., Scotch-Brite) to prevent mineral buildup from causing false "full" or "empty" signals.Vacuum Breakers: Standard on rinse lines to prevent backflow; these require regular inspection and use of repair kits (e.g., Hobart 00-292910) if the internal float fails to seal.Delime Cycle: When the delime notification appears, the operator must add delimer and run the automated cycle. On Advansys models, this cycle includes the booster heater (Booster Guard), which is critical for maintaining heating efficiency.Diagnostic Codes and TroubleshootingHobart controls provide advanced diagnostics via the user interface. Error codes are used to identify mechanical or sensor failures.Error Code / SymptomEngineering InterpretationAction Required"Empty Chemical" IconSensor detects no chemical in line for 20 seconds of priming. Verify chemical level; reset prime via Manager Menu. Flashing TempTemperature is below NSF minimum set-point. Check heater relays; verify incoming water temp. READY not displayedMachine is in preheat mode or water level not reached. Verify solenoid (SOL1) operation and PRS sensor signal. Blue Start flashingDoor switch (LS1) circuit is open during start attempt. Inspect door magnet (00-290160) and switch alignment. Standards Compliance and Technical SpecificationsAll Hobart dishmachines are engineered to comply with various international health, safety, and energy standards.NSF/ANSI Standard 3: This standard defines the requirements for commercial warewashing equipment, focusing on sanitization efficiency and cleanability of the machine's internal surfaces.FDA Food Code: Specifies the minimum wash and rinse temperatures (150°F wash, 160°F dish surface temp for sanitization).DIN 10512: European standard for commercial dishwashing performance. PREMAX units are specifically engineered to meet these cycle time and temperature profiles.HACCP Compliance: The integration of SmartConnect and USB data interfaces allows for "Intelligent Data Documentation." The machines automatically record operational data, temperature logs, and consumption rates, providing a digital protocol for hygiene compliance.Energy Star: Most current Hobart models are Energy Star 3.0 certified, signifying a minimum of 25% better energy and water efficiency than non-certified models.This engineering knowledge base serves as a technical record of Hobart’s product architecture. From the 40-gallon modules of the FT series to the 0.62-gallon high-efficiency rinse of the LXn, the focus remains on hydraulic optimization, thermal recycling through DWER systems, and robust electronic control through modular PCB architecture. The documentation of legacy models like the AM15 and CL44e provides the historical technical context required for the ongoing maintenance of the existing machine population.
